So what he's saying is that just Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala) "infused" the spirit with the body to bring it to life, we have to infuse the teachings of revelation within the intellect to bring it to life. A mere connection between the two is not enough. Let me give you a practical example to clarify this for you. 


So for example, I know that the 5 daily prayers are extremely beneficial for my soul. I know that! I have that knowledge! But that does not necessarily mean that I will pray 5 times a day, right? Why is that? I have the necessary knowledge, that should be good enough to make me act, right? But it doesn't work that way! We all know that it is great and awesome to be courageous, kind, loving, generous - we all have that knowledge, but we do not necessarily practice them, right? Why? Because that knowledge is simply connected with our intellects. We can talk about it! We can discuss it! We can innumerate it's benefits! We can write papers and essays about it! We can carry that knowledge everywhere we go with us - but that knowledge is not a part of our intellects, you understand? It hasn't infused with our intellects yet!

But then we know of people who never ever skipped their prayers. There are people who would rather die before they miss a prayer. Imam Hussain in Karbala, right? Every محرم we hear the story of مسلم بن عقیل. What did he say when his executioner was about to throw him face down off the roof? When he asked مسلم بن عقیل for his last wish, what did he say? Allow me to pray 2 رکعات! Allow me to pray! Why? He's on the threshold of death. He's going to meet Allah in a matter of minutes! Then why is he asking to pray? And what about his two sons? محمد and ابراھیم? When حارس لعن اللہ علیہ was about to behead them, what did they say to him? If you have decided to kill us then let us offer 2 رکعات prayers before you do so.

So what's the difference here? What's the difference between them and us? Is it that they understand the importance of prayer and we don't? No! At an intellectual level we both understand the importance of prayer. The difference is that our knowledge of صلوۃ is simply connected with our intellects, whereas for them, that knowledge had infused with their intellects. It was an integral part of their existence that could not be separated anymore. 

And this is something we can appreciate from our own experiences in other areas of our lives. Those of you are very health and hygiene conscious, you just cannot tolerate any kind of filth. You just cannot stop yourself from cleaning that stain on the toilet sink, and it's not even your house! You're a guest in someone else's house!    Some cannot fall asleep unless they floss and brush their teeth for 30 minutes, right? Why? Because that cleanliness, that hygiene has permeated your spirit. It has infused within your intellect! Your intellect is alive with that spirit of cleanliness. You understand?


From Body To Spirit

Now here's something I want you reflect upon. Did Allah give Adam (AS) a body first? Or did he give him a spirit first? From the Quranic verses, and the statements of Maulana Ali (AS), we see that Allah gave Adam a body first and finally when it was completely ready, he breathed his spirit into him. Yes? 


There are many people who say that I'll pray after I establish a connection with Allah! What's the point of standing in prayer without that deep spiritual connection? This is how Satan gets us, you see? 


EXERCISE:
I want you all to do this exercise with me. All of you put your hands together palms facing each other. Kids! Do this with me and make sure your parents do it as well. OK? Put your palms together and rub them as hard as you can. Do it for a few seconds. Do they feel warm? Now think about how you generated this warmth in your hands? By working; by rubbing them together. Look at any phenomena in this world. The physical work always comes first; and the fruits of that labor come as a result of that physical effort.
So if you say, "I will pray once I establish this deep connection with Allah", that like you sitting outside on a chilly night saying, I'll rub my hands together when they feel warm. The act of SALAAT is the physical effort that generates the warmth of connection with Allah. So if you're waiting for that connecting to develop first, before you put in the hard work; you'll be sitting there waiting a long long time.



IQALB

So what the author is saying, is that the 3 components of the spirit of the intellect (روح العقل) are like the body. So you have to first develop the body just like Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala) first created Adam's body. We have to use our intellects to understand the Quranic reasons. We have to center our life around the principles of revelation and we have to harmonize our intellects with it by submitting to the teachings of Rasul Allah(Salallahu alayhi wa aalihee wa sallam) and Maulana Ali (AS). That is going to create the body, the exterior of our existence. When we keep working on it with sincerity and devotion, and once that external shell of our worship and servitude is ready, Allah will breath into it his spirit, which will bring it to life - just as we saw in the case of Adam (AS). You see how this all connects? 


So there it is! This was the topic of روح العقل. Allah created us with an intellect (العقل) and he revealed his intentions to us through the Quran. He revealed theظاھر, the تنزیل through Rasul Allah(Salallahu alayhi wa aalihee wa sallam); and he revealed its باطن its تاویل, through Maulana Ali (AS). Why did he send us these messengers? To unearth the buried treasures of our intellects. To remind us what we have forgotten - الست بربکم؟ Am I not your master? بلی شھدنا Indeed we testify that you are! That is what they are reminding us of. How are they reminding us? Through signs (آیات). Allah created this universe for us on the exact same pattern of the Quran, so that we can study his physical signs and align them with his Quranic signs (his آیات). Our intellects absorb revelation in the form of knowledge through its words and meaning; and it then validates and confirms the authenticity of that knowledge through the empirical and rational observations found in the horizons and the soul. This generates faith in our heart. Through that faith, we put the knowledge of the Quran into practice, with understanding and comprehension of why we're doing what we doing - and through our sincere and consistent practice, we infuse that Quranic knowledge, and its teachings within our spirit. And the portion of our spirit that gets "in-spired" and "infused" with that Quranic knowledge, to the degree that it can no longer be separated from it is what's called the spiritual heart or قلب. 

And it is this spiritual heart, according to Maulana Ali (AS), that then becomes capable of witnessing Allah. The spiritual heart (قلب)  is the "alive" portion of the spirit, that is capable of witnessing Allah, you see? A dead person cannot witness anything! You have to be alive to be able to witness something. This is why it is the spiritual heart, and not the intellect that is capable of witnessing Allah. Quran says:

QThe Heights [7:179]



لَهُمْ قُلُوبٌۭ لَّا يَفْقَهُونَ بِهَا

They have hearts with which they do not understand. Allah does not say, "They have intellects that do not understand!" He says they have hearts that do not understand.  Why is that? Because intellectually they understand everything. They understand that this is the true message and the right path! But they are unable to infuse it into their hearts, you see? These are the people the Quran refers to as منافقون - the hypocrites. In سورۃ منافقون he says:

QAl-Munafiqun [Q


‏ذَلِكَ بِأَنَّهُمْ آمَنُوا۟ ثُمَّ كَفَرُوا۟

That is because they believed, then disbelieved. What does that mean? How do you believe and then disbelieve at the same time? Because they believe at the intellectual level, but then they do not make the effort to infuse those teachings within the spirit. They do not practice what they learn. So what does Allah do?

فَطُبِعَ عَلَىٰ قُلُوبِهِمْ

As a punishment, Allah seals their hearts! Not their intellects! Their hearts! 

فَهُمْ لَا يَفْقَهُونَ

So now they can no longer understand. Allah seals their hearts as a punishment. He closes the doors of understanding on them. You see?


So what Maulana Ali (AS) is essentially saying, is that a life, in the absence of Allah's spirit; because remember! Allah didn't just breadth "a" spirit into us. He put in "his" spirit within us:

QThe Prostration [32:7-9]


‏ثُمَّ سَوَّىٰهُ وَنَفَخَ فِيهِ مِن رُّوحِهِ

Then He fashioned him in due proportion, and breathed into him from his spirit. I'll explain what this means in detail when we resume our wise reminder sessions. But for now, just keep this in mind - that Allah breathed his spirit into Adam; into us. So Maulana Ali (AS) says that a life in the absence of the intention, to be true to Allah's spirit; to be true to what Allah wants from us in this life, is also a kind of death. So just as by the spirit, this energy,  brings inanimate and unconscious matter to life and gives it an awareness of itself; it is through the spirit of Allah; by being true to the spirit of Allah; by living a life that is aligned with the intentions and will of Allah; through the essence of توحید, that our intellects truly come to life.  Just as Allah infused his spirit into our body to bring it to life, in the same way we have to infuse the inner meanings and teachings of Quran, explained through the interpretations of Maulana Ali (AS), in order to enliven our worship and our spiritual existence. So when we talk about spirit vs body, it is not just a matter of contrasting the spirit with the body, but rather it is the realization, that the infusion of the spirit into the body is what essentially brings it to life. This is why spiritual knowledge enlivens the hearts; not physical knowledge. You can study all the physics, chemistry, biology, mathematics of the world! It's not going to bring your heart to life! The only thing that will enliven your heart is the spiritual knowledge of the Quran; because it is the heart, and not the intellect that according to Quran, can witness the beatific vision of Allah.


This is why the Quran was revealed to us - so we can once again witness Allah and testify for his ربوبیۃ in this physical realm, just as we did in the spiritual realm. If that were not the goal, then revelation would be nothing more than a bunch of rules and regulations for the intellect, rather than being a source of creative inspiration for the heart. Therefore divine revelation requires a creative engagement with its teachings, utilizing all the resources of the intellect and the heart. The goal of Quran is not to impose some some unintelligible decree on us, that cuts short the operations of reason. It's quite the opposite! The goal is to utilize the multiplicity of reason to develop an overall, unified concept, and then infuse that into the spiritual heart.  

IExplosion of meaning

This is why the Quran constantly demands فکر - a deep reflection. Because what this deep reflection does, is that it creates a strong relationship between the intellect and the revelation, excluding all superficiality. Once reflection comes into play, the recitation of Quran is not longer a flat, unilateral reading that confines its meanings to a literal text. You see? Rather than enclosing and confining meaning, reflection actually discloses it, it opens it up; it exposes the meanings, it reveals them. There is an enormous expansion that explodes and opens up the inner horizons of multiple levels of meaning that were hidden in the literal text. It makes the meaning multi-dimensional that can now be applied to all areas of our lives - both دنیا as well as آخرۃ. 


Intellect & Justice

The author concludes by saying that being 'spiritual', essentially means, being true to one's intellect. Being true to the treasures buried deep within it; and not just to the rational functions operating on its surface. In the eyes of Maulana Ali, the 'true intellectual' (what he considers العاقل)  is the one who not only 'thinks' correctly but also 'acts' ethically. In his view, a true intellect is the one who at the deepest level seeks to 'realize' the ultimate Reality. This is why he defines an "intellectual" as the one who puts all things in their proper place'. 

العاقل من وضع شئی حق محلہ

The intellect is the one who put things in its proper place. Who aligns his actions with the intentions of his creator, you see? Now what's really interesting is that this is also the very definition of justice (عدل). That's what justice means - When you put things in their proper place, you're being just - you're being عادل. 

WAQIL vs ADIL

Look at the two words عاقل and عادل. They're exactly the same except for قاف and دال. And you should know by now that when two words are similar in spelling, they have a very strong relationship between them. And the relationship here is that you have to be a عاقل in order to be a عادل. Maulana Ali (AS) says, that only a true intellectual can be fully just. Because only the only one who has  the ability to see things as they truly are, is able to put them in  their proper place. Therefore, thought, action and realization, are all concerns of the true intellectual -العاقل. 

I3 Components Summation

This is why the author states, that Maulana Ali (AS) is the greatest عادل - he was the most just human being to walk the face of this earth - because he was the most عاقل. He was the greatest intellectual, you see? And if you study the spiritual ethos of Maulana Ali (AS),  you'll see that his entire life was a manifestation of correct thinking, impeccable virtue, and authentic being - all inseparably tied up with his intellect - making him the perfect intellectual عاقل, the most knowledgeable عالم, and the most just عادل.  And this should be target of our lives as well. 





TAWHEED


So Maulana Ali (AS), right then and there, on the battleground of جمل gives an incredible خطبۃ signifying the oneness of Allah - a oneness, that he says cannot fall into the category of numbers. Now what the author highlights for us in his discussion, is the highly paradoxical nature of the statements he uses to reveal the oneness of Allah. Things like, eyes cannot see him, but the minds cannot deny him either. He is so distant that the flights of imaginations cannot reach him, but closer to us than our jugular vein. And this is something that is very typical about the sermons of Maulana Ali (AS).  It almost seems that the higher the  truth is, the deeper paradox he creates to reveal it. Why? Because each human conception of reality needs to be 'deconstructed' first in order to then 'reconstruct'. So the paradoxes that Maulana Ali creates, they feed directly into the modes of spiritual realizations, that reason on its own account is incapable of attaining. So he literally dismembers our understanding of Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala) through these paradoxes, and then engages our contemplation, which 're-members' or 'reconstructs' these spiritual realities - realities that transcend the limitations of reason. So what happens is that through the union of these such paradox, these opposing forces, a new reality emerges in our consciousness. A reality that does not negate reason, but rather builds it in a new way that allows us to truly 'comprehend' these higher spiritual realities, that we would never be able to understand or express fully otherwise. 


So when Maulana Ali negates the possibility of human conceptions encompassing the divine Reality, what he is negating is our ability to conceptually understand him. That's what he's negating. He's negating the limitations of our words and concepts; and through that negation, he is actually revealing the positive content - which is the معنی, the spiritual meaning of the concept itself. You see? Because reason creates limitations - it "de-fines" an idea. It creates a boundary (a حد). Through these paradoxical statements, he breaks that boundary for us, to engage a  deeper level of awareness than extends far beyond the domain of speculative thought. In other words, through the negation of the limitations of our reason, he affirms the the Absolute, who has no limitations. So when he says, human minds cannot understand Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala), by expressing the limitations of our human intellect, he constructs the limitlessness and the absoluteness of the real. And nowhere is this style of discourse more apparent  than in the famous opening lines of the first sermon of نھج البلاغہ: 


الحَمْدُ للهِ الَّذَي لاَ يَبْلُغُ مِدْحَتَهُ القَائِلُونَ

Praise be to Allah, whose praise those who speak cannot deliver. So there's the paradox of speech and the inability to deliver. Because that's what speech is meant to do. It is meant to deliver a concept. So you see how he deconstructs the ability of speech to construct the idea that Allah is so lofty and so sublime, that it is impossible for words to express anything about him.

وَلاِ يُحْصِي نَعْمَاءَهُ العَادُّونَ

whose graces those who count cannot enumerate; because counting is used for enumeration, but not when it comes to Allah's bounties.

ولاَ يُؤَدِّي حَقَّهُ الُمجْتَهِدُونَ

and those who strive, no matter how hard they strive, they can never give him his due of praise and gratitude

الَّذِي لاَ يُدْركُهُ بُعْدُ الهِمَمِ

He cannot be grasped by  aspirations

وَلاَ يَنَالُهُ غَوْصُ الفِطَنِ

nor can he be fathomed by profound intuitions

الَّذِي لَيْسَ لِصِفَتِهِ حَدٌّ مَحْدُودٌ، وَلاَ نَعْتٌ مَوْجُودٌ، وَلا وَقْتٌ مَعْدُودٌ

He whose attributes have no binding limitation; there is no description that can capture or express who he is; and there is no time or duration that could capture his duration. Therefore his divine attributes (صفۃ) cannot be confined to a definition; and yet it is affirmed as real. It is موصوف, it is described by the very words of the Quranic revelation - even though it has no  'description'. Isn't that interesting? 

For example, Allah is described as having attributes such as, He is intensely merciful (رحمن), he is consistently merciful (رحیم). He is عزیز  - the Almighty, and so on. But Maulana Ali affirms that these very attributes cannot be defined,  bounded, or described. Yes he is merciful, but merciful like who? Like you? Like me? Like Him? Like Them? Yes he is powerful, but like what? Like a storm? Like a flood? Like a tsunami? Because we have a mental concepts of these powerful forces. We are able to define these images through movies, or experience. So yes we know what powerful means and feels like; and Allah is all powerful, but not like these things. Then how? What is he powerful like? We don't know. We will never know, because we are incapable of knowing! We are limited in our knowing. SubhanAllah! 

And this is why the sermons of Maulana Ali (AS) are so focused on the perfection of أخلاص. The word أخلاص means 'rendering sincere', or 'rendering pure'. And it means the purification of one's devotion to Allah, and ones conception of Allah. It means to purify, and divest Allah of all attributes (نفی الصفۃ) . So on one hand, the attributes of Allah are affirmed and understood in terms of they mean; but then on the other they are to be completely negated. 


Now the thing is, the rational mind can only  think of Allah in terms of these attributes that are verbally expressed in the  Quran. Yet here, Maulana Ali appears to negate these same attributes, thereby depriving the mind of any convenient conceptual handle by which  to grasp the nature of the divine Reality. And so what this does, is that this simultaneous  affirmation, and negation of the attributes, generates an apparently  irresolvable contradiction in our minds. You see?  

Powerful, but now powerful like you understand what power is! Merciful but not how you understand the concept of mercy! You see? 

And in doing so, what he does is that he enhances our receptivity to a deeper mode of knowing. He enables us to understand Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala) and know him in a much more deeper way! This awareness then invites the seeker to "dive" into the depths of knowledge.  It's like an electric shock. This very  shock of the apparent contradiction, generates a leap of consciousness from the formal activity of reason, to the super-rational contemplation of the spirit.


In the خطبۃ that he gives to the bedouin he says that my heart had indeed witnessed Allah,  but not by means of outward vision (بصارۃ) but through 'the realities of faith'; through insight (through بصیرۃ). He says, my Lord is not described in terms of dimension (بُعْد), nor motion (حرکۃ), nor rest (سکون)  nor through coming or going. He is subtle in subtlety, but he is not described  by subtlety. He is magnificent in magnificence but he is not described by  magnificence. He is great in greatness, but not described by greatness. He again affirms and denies Allah's attributes in the same sentence. He highlights, that Allah possesses these attributes, but you cannot reduce him to your understanding of these attributes. So we can begin to think of Allah in terms of these categories, but we cannot reduce Him to our limited conceptions of these attributes. The purpose of these attributes, is merely to afford something to the mind by which the divine nature can be understood. But this understanding is fragmented, never exhaustive. It therefore needs to be negated, Why? So that a fuller understanding of these attributes can be generated! Therefore our initial conceptual understanding of the divine Reality needs to be both negated and sublimated. It needs to be negated as being a mental conception, while the positive content of the conception needs to be elevated to a high rank through spiritual intuition. Only then can the two-dimensional  'portrait' defined by reason, be given the third dimension of depth. But  this third dimension emerges only after the insufficiency of the rational depiction is clearly understood. Paradox produces this  cognitive shock, which reveals the pretension of reason in its effort to  disclose the nature of the Absolute.   


So Maulana Ali (AS) uses the combination of affirmation and denial in his discourses, which mirrors the negation and affirmation found in the principle testimony of Islam: لا الہ, 'No deity', being the negation (نفی); followed by الا اللہ, 'except Allah' coming as the affirmation (اثبات). And so by the negation of all deities and the affirmation of the one true deity, the perfection of اخلاص. is constructed. So according to Maulana Ali (AS), the perfection of أخلاص is gained by the negation of the  attributes 'in order to' affirm the infinitude of the divine Reality.  Thus, each of the attributes is to be 'negated', but only insofar as it  is conceived by the limited mind. 


Therefore negation is necessary,  because at the mental level each attribute 'excludes' not only the other  attributes, but also the object of attribution itself (the divine  Essence). For any concept to develop in the mind, defined ideas are  required. And where there are definitions, there are limits, which  result in exclusions. However, the reality of the attribute is unlimited  by virtue of its absolute identity with the Essence. The attribute that  has been 'negated' is paradoxically affirmed as being nothing 'but'  Allah: illa Allah. Thus, one can place each of the negated attributes in the place of the negation (nafy) of the first testimony, and arrive at such formulations as: La Rahmana illa'Llah, 'There is no All-Compassionate except Allah'; La Raheema illa'Llah, 'There is No All-Merciful except Allah'.

 In this respect, the apparent negation of the attributes not only  becomes an affirmation of their true identity, but it also disconnects  any restrictive association with the poverty of mental conceptions. The  attributes are not super-imposed upon the Essence, but are in fact  absolutely one with it. The attributes are differentiable from the  essence, only in conception and not in existence. The Essence does  indeed comprise all the mentioned attributes such as greatness,  subtlety, and so on, but it is also infinitely more than the sum of  these attributes. These attributes are infinite, only within and as the  Essence. Maulana Ali's explanation of this paradox here, can be seen in  fact as a commentary of the Quranic verse:

 

   

   
 سورۃ المؤمنون
 
 { 23:91 }
 
  ‏سُبْحَنَ ٱللَّهِ عَمَّا يَصِفُونَ ‎   91
   
  Glorified be Allah above all that they attribute to Him! 91
 

 

 The infinitude of Allah, requires the negation of all that the mind  can conceive through reason on the human plane. But it does not negate  all that the heart can 'see' through spiritual vision. This very  negation on the plane of reason calls out to something deeper. This in  turn, can only be understood in the light of spiritual practice, which  is always assumed as the reflective, devotional or contemplative context  for the hearers of Maulana Ali's sermons and discourses. The heart,  which is the symbolic seat of the contemplative intelligence, calls out  to be 'polished', and this is precisely what the 'remembrance of Allah'  does. This is why the rememberance of Allah (Zikr Allah) according to Maulana Ali, is the 'most excellent form of worship'.

   Interestingly, even at the level of practice there is a further  paradox. Because the 'reality of the remembrance' is not attained until  'you forget yourself' in your remembrance of Allah. It is not just the  rational mind and its concepts that are to be negated or  'deconstructed'. Instead, it is the individual 'soul' itself (as the  locus of all cognition, rational or supra-rational), that is to be  negated or 'deconstrcuted'. This final denouement, or annihilation (fana), is the ultimate form of paradox.

After explaining the spirituality of the intellect, the author next looks at a discourse, a خطبۃ that Maulana Ali (AS) gave to one of his companions كُميل بن زياد النخعي, This discourse is recorded in نھج البلاغۃ. The author does an in depth analysis of a small portion of this discourse, to really highlight the spiritual ethos of Maulana Ali (AS). To bring to light how deep his thought process was, and essentially how he truly perceived religion. The goal in that, I believe, is that we adopt that same thought process and train ourselves to look at religion in the same light that Maulana Ali (AS) saw it in. 


Before we begin, just a quick intro of كُميل بن زياد النخعي.  He was among the most loyal companions of Maulana Ali AS. He was originally from Yemen. He converted to Islam during the time of Rasul Allah(Salallahu alayhi wa aalihee wa sallam) when he was 10 years old. Historians state that Rasul Allah(Salallahu alayhi wa aalihee wa sallam) sent Maulana Ali (AS) to Yemen to guide them to Islam. He reached Yemen in the afternoon and gave such a powerful خطبۃ that the entire Yemen become Muslim by مغرب. AllahuAkbar! كُميل بن زياد النخعي, was among the children who converted to Islam on that day. He is recognized in Islam for his very pious and humble nature, but more so for preserving Maulana Ali's teachings. He was one of the students and companions of Maulana Ali, who also gave him the supplication of خضر علیہ السلام which after that day became known as دعاء الکمیل. 


When Maulana Ali (AS) came into power as the fourth caliph كُميل بن زياد, was among his greatest supporters. During that time Maulana Ali (AS) opened his doors of knowledge to a few of his selected companions such as مالک اشتر ، عویث قرنی and كُميل بن زياد. ; because not everyone had the capacity to handle his knowledge.

By his own narration, one day he was riding with Maulana Ali, and being a student of Maulana Ali for some time, he began feeling closer to the realities of truth. So as they were riding, he asks Ali (AS), What is your truth about the realities of Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala)? In other words, Tell me everything you know about Allah! Ali asks him, What makes you think you can understand that reality? كُميل replies to him, "Am I not your companion in your secrets?". In other words, كُميل felt he has received such a great share of knowledge from Maulana Ali that he is worthy of being called his companion in his secrets of Allah. Ali looked at him and said:

بلی! ولکن مَا یَرْشَحُ عَلَیکَ یُطْفَحُ مِنِّی

"Indeed! But what sprinkles in you, overflows within me". SubhanAllah! What sprinkles in you, overflows within me! In other words, the knowledge that you possess about Allah is like a sprinkle of water, whereas the knowledge I possess about him is like an ocean in comparison.


Today I want to talk about a famous discourse narrated by كُميل بن زياد. This discourse has been the subject of numerous commentaries through the centuries. Its true importance lies, among other things, in its highly esoteric character. It demonstrates the ethos of Maulana Ali's spirituality and the depths of his knowledge. He states in نھج البلاغۃ that

رأسُ الفضائل العلم؛ و غایۃ الفضائل العلم

The fountainhead of all virtue is knowledge; and the peak of all virtue is knowledge. In other words, the best of all deeds is to gain knowledge. In another tradition he says:

العلم الحیاۃ

Knowledge is life; which is very interesting, because he is essentially saying that if you don't have knowledge, then you're not truly alive. Knowledge creates a wholesome human being. We cannot rise to higher levels of knowledge and awareness of Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala) without knowledge. And this discourse of كُميل بن زيادis basically about knowledge and how to acquire this knowledge.


Discourse To Kumayl

كُميل بن زياد states that one day Maulana Ali (AS) caught hold of my hand and took me to the graveyard. When he had passed through the graveyard and left the city behind, he took a deep sigh and said: 

يَا كُمَيْل بْن زِيَادٍ، إِنَّ هذهِ الْقُلُوبَ أَوْعِيَةٌ فَخَيْرُهَا أَوْعَاهَا

O كُميل Indeed these hearts are vessels or containers. Containers for what? For knowledge and wisdom! O كُميل Indeed these hearts are vessels for knowledge and wisdom. فَخَيْرُهَا أَوْعَاهَا and the best of them are those which hold the most. 

فَاحْفَظْ عَنِّي مَا أَقُولُ لَكَ

So take and preserve from me what I say to you. Just this first sentence opens up a world of reflection for us. We live in an age where we are constantly bombarded with so much information all the time - most of which is just sheer nonsense. The question we have to ask ourselves here, is what are we filling our vessels with? What type of content do we preserve in our hearts? Because every container has its limits, right? There is only so much a container can hold; and if we fill it with the wrong stuff, there is no room to then put the right content in. Then he says:

النَّاسُ ثَلاَثَةٌ- فَعَالِمٌ رَبَّانِيٌّ وَمُتَعَلِّمٌ عَلَى سَبِيلِ نَجَاةٍ، وَهَمَجٌ رَعَاعٌ أَتْبَاعُ كُلِّ نَاعِقٍ يَمِيلُونَ مَعَ كُلِّ رِيحٍ، لَمْ يَسْتَضِيئُوا بِنُورِ الْعِلْمِ، وَلَمْ يَلْجَؤُوا إِلَى رُكْنٍ وَثِيقٍ


He says: النَّاسُ ثَلاَثَةٌ - People are of three types: فَعَالِمٌ رَبَّانِيٌّ - First is the scholar who is well versed in the philosophies of religion and the intricacies of truth. These are people who have spent years of their life learning these علوم الالھیۃ under a qualified teacher, and achieved mastery and perfection in these fields of spiritual knowledge. 

وَمُتَعَلِّمٌ عَلَى سَبِيلِ نَجَاةٍ

The second type are those, who are students; people that are seeking that divine and spiritual knowledge for the purpose of their salvation and deliverance.

وَهَمَجٌ رَعَاعٌ أَتْبَاعُ كُلِّ نَاعِقٍ يَمِيلُونَ مَعَ كُلِّ رِيحٍ  

And the rest are the هَمَجٌ, which is a moth. These are people who gets attracted by any and every flame. These are people who who run towards anyone who calls them, and they bend in whichever direction the wind blows; swaying with every current

لَمْ يَسْتَضِيئُوا بِنُورِ الْعِلْمِ، وَلَمْ يَلْجَؤُوا إِلَى رُكْنٍ وَثِيقٍ  

They have no desire to be illumined by the light of knowledge, nor do they take protection of any reliable support. These are spiritually aimless people who are completely drowned in the acquisition of دنیا and have absolutely no concerns for their afterlife. They are ships without a rudder, sailing in whichever direction the wind blows. So Maulana Ali (AS) says to كُميل, be from the first 2 categories of people and not from the third. The ideal station is to be from the first group who are well versed in the ethics of religion. If you cannot do that, then at least be a student, seeking the path of salvation and deliverance. But never be from the third group who are like the هَمَجٌ- absolutely without any purpose or use.  Then he says:

يَا كُمَيْلُ، الْعِلْمُ خَيْرٌ مِنَ الْمَالِ؛ الْعِلْمُ يَحْرُسُكَ وَأَنْتَ تَحْرُسُ المَالَ؛ وَالْمَالُ تَنْقُصُهُ النَّفَقَةُ وَالْعِلْمُ يَزْكُوعَلَى الاْنْفَاقِ ؛ وَصَنِيعُ الْمَالِ يَزُولُ بِزَوَالِهِ

Now he creates a comparison between knowledge and wealth. Why these two entities? Because they are both currencies for a successful life. Wealth is the key to success in the physical world; knowledge is the key to success in the afterlife. But there is always a trade-off; because they both require time. You have to spend time earning both; which means you will have to sacrifice one for the other. So now he gives کمیل (and us) the pros and cons to help us make a decision as to which currency to choose. He says:

يَا كُمَيْلُ، الْعِلْمُ خَيْرٌ مِنَ الْمَالِ 

O كُميل, knowledge is better than wealth. In other words, if you're faced with a choice between gaining knowledge and gaining wealth, choose knowledge - why? Because

الْعِلْمُ يَحْرُسُكَ وَأَنْتَ تَحْرُسُ المَالَ

Because knowledge guards you, while you have to guard your wealth

وَالْمَالُ تَنْقُصُهُ النَّفَقَةُ، وَالْعِلْمُ يَزْكُوعَلَى الاْنْفَاقِ

And because wealth decreases when you spend it; whereas knowledge increases when you share it. In other words, wealth will decrease through spending, but knowledge will always replenish itself with more, when disbursed.

وَصَنِيعُ الْمَالِ يَزُولُ بِزَوَالِهِ

and the results of wealth disappear with the disappearance of wealth. All the friends one has in their life disappear as soon as they lose their money. All the things we purchased with money, we have to give them up or sell them when we can no longer sustain that lifestyle. But nobody can take our knowledge and wisdom away. Then he says:

يَا كُمَيْلَ بْنَ زِيَادٍ! مَعْرِفَةُ الْعِلْمِ دِينٌ يُدَانُ بِهِ؛ بِهِ يَكْسِبُ الْإِنْسَانُ الطَّاعَةَ فِى حَيَاتِهِ وَ جَمِيلَ الْأُحْدُوثَةِ بَعْدَ وَفَاتِهِ ؛ وَ الْعِلْمُ حَاكِمٌ وَ الْمَالُ مَحْكُومٌ عَلَيْهِ

Then he says:

يَا كُمَيْلَ بْنَ زِيَادٍ مَعْرِفَةُ الْعِلْمِ دِينٌ يُدَانُ بِهِ

The wise apprehension of knowledge is a religion by which (Allah) is worshipped. Acquisition of knowledge is the repayment of the debt owed to Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala). The word دین is related to دَین which means debt. We owe it to Allah to recognize him. What made us incur that debt? Our Intellects! So how do we repay this debt of معرفۃ and طاعۃ? By acquiring knowledge.

بِهِ يَكْسِبُ الْإِنْسَانُ الطَّاعَةَ فِى حَيَاتِهِ وَ جَمِيلَ الْأُحْدُوثَةِ بَعْدَ وَفَاتِهِ

Through it, man acquires obedience in his life and a good name after his death.

وَ الْعِلْمُ حَاكِمٌ وَ الْمَالُ مَحْكُومٌ عَلَيْهِ

And knowledge is a judge, while wealth is judged. In other words, knowledge is active, whereas wealth is passive. The one with knowledge can take control of things in his life, because his knowledge guides him in how to handle those things; whereas wealth by its nature is always something that is ruled upon by the one who possesses it.

يَا كُمَيْلُ هَلَكَ خُزَّانُ الْأَمْوَالِ وَ هُمْ أَحْيَاءٌ وَ الْعُلَمَاءُ بَاقُونَ مَا بَقِيَ الدَّهْرُ أَعْيَانُهُمْ مَفْقُودَةٌ وَ أَمْثَالُهُمْ فِى الْقُلُوبِ مَوْجُودَةٌ هَا إِنَّ هَاهُنَا لَعِلْماً جَمّاً وَ أَشَارَ بِيَدِهِ إِلَى صَدْرِهِ لَوْ أَصَبْتُ لَهُ حَمَلَةً بَلَى أَصَبْتُ لَقِناً غَيْرَ مَأْمُونٍ عَلَيْهِ مُسْتَعْمِلًا آلَةَ الدِّينِ لِلدُّنْيَا وَ مُسْتَظْهِراً بِنِعَمِ اللَّهِ عَلَى عِبَادِهِ وَ بِحُجَجِهِ عَلَى أَوْلِيَائِهِ أَوْ مُنْقَاداً لِحَمَلَةِ الْحَقِّ لَا بَصِيرَةَ لَهُ فِى أَحْنَائِهِ يَنْقَدِحُ الشَّكُّ فِى قَلْبِهِ لِأَوَّلِ عَارِضٍ مِنْ شُبْهَةٍ      

Then he reveals the legacy of knowledge. He says:

يَا كُمَيْلُ هَلَكَ خُزَّانُ الْأَمْوَالِ وَ هُمْ أَحْيَاءٌ

O كُمَيْلُ, those who hoard wealth perish even while they are alive

وَ الْعُلَمَاءُ بَاقُونَ مَا بَقِيَ الدَّهْرُ

but people of knowledge endure for as long as time subsists; 

أَعْيَانُهُمْ مَفْقُودَةٌ وَ أَمْثَالُهُمْ فِى الْقُلُوبِ مَوْجُودَةٌ

their (material) forms may be absent, but their (spiritual) images live on forever in the hearts of people. Now here we see the psychological wisdom of taking کمیل through the graveyard. He established the proof of his argument before he made the argument. Then he points to his chest and says:

إِنَّ هَاهُنَا لَعِلْماً جَمّاً

Within this chest lies an abundance of knowledge

لَوْ أَصَبْتُ لَهُ حَمَلَةً

If only I could find someone to bear it 

بَلَى أَصَبْتُ

Yes, I found one who was quick to understand,

لَقِناً غَيْرَ مَأْمُونٍ عَلَيْهِ

but he could not be trusted with it,

مُسْتَعْمِلًا آلَةَ الدِّينِ لِلدُّنْيَا وَ مُسْتَظْهِراً بِنِعَمِ اللَّهِ عَلَى عِبَادِهِ وَ بِحُجَجِهِ عَلَى أَوْلِيَائِهِ

he would exploit the tools of religion for the sake of his own worldly gains. He would use this knowledge to empower himself with the graces of Allah against His slaves, and with His proofs against His friends. 

Now he's talking about the dangers of true knowledge falling into the wrong hands. If the vessel of the heart is corrupt, then this same knowledge - despite its purity and value, will be used for personal gains and to hurt the friends of Allah. This is why we do not share our علوم with the corrupt of heart. Not because we don't want to propagate the truth, but because we know that not only will this knowledge not benefit such people, they will in fact turn against the friends of Allah to protect their own interests.

أَوْ مُنْقَاداً لِحَمَلَةِ الْحَقِّ لَا بَصِيرَةَ لَهُ فِى أَحْنَائِهِ

Then there was one who was obedient to the bearers of truth, but lacked heartfelt insight. Why is such a person not fit for knowledge of higher realities? 

يَنْقَدِحُ الشَّكُّ فِى قَلْبِهِ لِأَوَّلِ عَارِضٍ مِنْ شُبْهَةٍ

Because at the first appearance of obscurity, doubt would kindle in his heart.

أَلَا لَا ذَا وَ لَا ذَاكَ! أَوْ مَنْهُوماً بِاللَّذَّةِ سَلِسَ الْقِيَادِ لِلشَّهْوَةِ || أَوْ مُغْرَماً بِالْجَمْعِ وَ الِادِّخَارِ || 
لَيْسَا مِنْ رُعَاةِ الدِّينِ فِى شَيْ‏ءٍ || أَقْرَبُ شَيْ‏ءٍ شَبَهاً بِهِمَا الْأَنْعَامُ السَّائِمَةُ || كَذَلِكَ يَمُوتُ الْعِلْمُ بِمَوْتِ حَامِلِيهِ 

He says:

أَلَا لَا ذَا وَ لَا ذَاكَ

So alas, neither this one nor that!  

أَوْ مَنْهُوماً بِاللَّذَّةِ سَلِسَ الْقِيَادِ لِلشَّهْوَةِ

The are either greedy for pleasures of this world and submissive to passion

أَوْ مُغْرَماً بِالْجَمْعِ وَ الِادِّخَارِ

or they are obsessed with acquisition and accumulation

لَيْسَا مِنْ رُعَاةِ الدِّينِ فِى شَيْ‏ءٍ

neither of them is a guardian of religion in any respect.

قْرَبُ شَيْ‏ءٍ شَبَهاً بِهِمَا الْأَنْعَامُ السَّائِمَةُ

They resemble nothing more than grazing cattle!  Now I find this analogy very interesting. They are nothing more than grazing cattle! Who are? People who are greedy for pleasure and submissive to passion; and those who are obsessed with accumulating and hoarding. Very interesting! That is what a low opinion Maulana Ali (AS) has of such people.

كَذَلِكَ يَمُوتُ الْعِلْمُ بِمَوْتِ حَامِلِيهِ

This is how knowledge dies away with the death of its bearers. When worthless or self-motivated people become leaders and knowledge bearers, that is what causes the death of knowledge.

اللَّهُمَّ بَلَى! لاَ تَخْلُو الاْرْضُ مِنْ قَائِمٍ لله بِحُجَّةٍ، إِمَّا ظَاهِراً مَشْهُوراً، أوْ خَائِفاً مَغْمُوراً لِئَلاَّ تَبْطُلَ حُجَجُ اللهِ وَبَيِّنَاتُهُ. وَكَمْ ذَا وَأَيْنَ أُولئِكَ؟ أُولئِكَ ـ وَاللَّهِ ـ الاْقَلُّونَ عَدَداً، وَالاْعْظَمُونَ قَدْراً، يَحْفَظُ اللهُ بِهِمْ حُجَجَهُ وَبَيِّنَاتِهِ، حَتَّى يُودِعُوهَا نُظَرَاءَهُمْ، وَيَزْرَعُوهَا فِي قُلُوبِ أَشْبَاهِهِمْ  


Then he says:

اللَّهُمَّ بَلَى!

But indeed! By Allah, Now he returns back to talking about the first category of people that he mentioned earlier - عَالِمٌ رَبَّانِيٌّ the lordly knower - the Imams. He begins describing the qualities of these Imams of truth... He says:

لاَ تَخْلُو الاْرْضُ مِنْ قَائِمٍ لله بِحُجَّةٍ، إِمَّا ظَاهِراً مَشْهُوراً، أوْ خَائِفاً مَغْمُوراً

The earth will never be empty of the one (Imam) who establishes the proof of Allah; whether he does it openly and publicly or fearfully in obscurity,

لِئَلاَّ تَبْطُلَ حُجَجُ اللهِ وَبَيِّنَاتُهُ

so that Allah's  proofs and elucidations  may never become extinct

وَكَمْ ذَا وَأَيْنَ أُولئِكَ؟

How many are they and where are they?

أُولئِكَ ـ وَاللَّهِ ـ الاْقَلُّونَ عَدَداً، وَالاْعْظَمُونَ قَدْراً

By Allah, they may be the smallest in number, but with Allah they are the greatest in rank. So here Maulana Ali (AS) is rejecting this concept of "majority is authority"; or the idea of "democracy" in religion. That just because a large number of people believe something to be true, therefore it must be right. In fact, he's saying the exact opposite. He's saying they are very few in number, but hold the greatest rank and station with Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala).

يَحْفَظُ اللهُ بِهِمْ حُجَجَهُ وَبَيِّنَاتِهِ

Through them Allah preserves His proofs and elucidations,

حَتَّى يُودِعُوهَا نُظَرَاءَهُمْ، وَيَزْرَعُوهَا فِي قُلُوبِ أَشْبَاهِهِمْ

so that they entrust them to their peers and sow that knowledge in the hearts of those resembling them.  

هَجَمَ بِهِمُ الْعِلْمُ عَلَى حَقِيقَةِ الْبَصِيرَةِ، وَبَاشَرُوا رُوحَ الْيَقِينِ، وَاسْتَلاَنُوا مَا اسْتَوْعَرَهُ الْمُتْرَفُونَ وَأَنِسُوا بِمَا اسْتَوْحَشَ مِنْهُ الْجَاهِلُونَ، وَصَحِبُوا الدُّنْيَا بِأَبْدَانٍ أَرْوَاحُهَا مُعَلَّقَةٌ بِالْـمَحَلِّ الاْعْلَى، أُولئِكَ خُلَفَاءُ اللهِ فِي أَرْضِهِ، وَالدُّعَاةُ إِلَى دِينِهِ، آهِ آهِ شَوْقاً إِلَى رُؤْيَتِهِمْ! انْصَرِفْ إذَا شِئْتَ  


هَجَمَ بِهِمُ الْعِلْمُ عَلَى حَقِيقَةِ الْبَصِيرَةِ

Through them, knowledge penetrates the reality of insight

وَبَاشَرُوا رُوحَ الْيَقِينِ

They rejoice in their intimacy with the spirit of certainty;

وَاسْتَلاَنُوامَا اسْتَوْعَرَهُ الْمُتْرَفُونَ 

they make easy what the extravagant find harsh; 

وَأَنِسُوا بِمَا اسْتَوْحَشَ مِنْهُ الْجَاهِلُونَ

they befriend that by which the ignorant are estranged.

وَصَحِبُوا الدُّنْيَا بِأَبْدَانٍ أَرْوَاحُهَا مُعَلَّقَةٌ بِالْـمَحَلِّ الاْعْلَى

With their bodies they keep company with the world, while their spirits are tied to the transcendent realm

أُولئِكَ خُلَفَاءُ اللهِ فِي أَرْضِهِ، وَالدُّعَاةُ إِلَى دِينِهِ

They are the vicegerents of Allah on His earth; and the ones who summon people to His religion
آهِ آهِ شَوْقاً إِلَى رُؤْيَتِهِمْ
Ah! how I long to see them! 

انْصَرِفْ إذَا شِئْتَ

Go now, Kumayl, as you will*
     

Spiritual Knowledge

For our purposes, I want to reflect upon the four sentences of this خطبۃ where he describes those who have the title of 'lordly scholars' (فَعَالِمٌ رَبَّانِيٌّ). He describes the qualities of these people towards the end of his خطبۃ where  he says that these are the ones who are very few in number but they are the greatest in the sight of Allah and it is through them that 'His religion' is sustained. What does he mean by His religion? He means, the divine reality of religion. They protect and preserve the pure essence of faith, which is not only perfect, but also immutable - the essence that does not change over time. Now this is in contrast to how we perceive religion in the general sense, right? Because from آدم to خاتم علیھم السلام, we see that the formal institutions of religion has changed based human conditions. But in reality the essence of religion, the message, the goal, the purpose of religion has always been the same - Salvation of the soul through willing submission to Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala) - as the Quran states:

إِنَّ ٱلدِّينَ عِندَ ٱللَّهِ ٱلْإِسْلَمُ

Iٰndeed The religion of Allah has always been submission to a higher will, for a higher purpose. So he says it is truly by means of these sages that the essence and spirit of religion truly 'lives' in the world. He then states a very peculiar quality of these beings

هَجَمَ بِهِمُ الْعِلْمُ عَلَى حَقِيقَةِ الْبَصِيرَةِ

Through them, knowledge penetrates the reality of insight. He did not say, their knowledge penetrates the reality of insight! He said, through them, knowledge penetrates the reality of insight. To explain this, the author then dives into the explanation of what he means by حَقِيقَةِ الْبَصِيرَةِ. He explains that it is possible for knowledge to exist as an abstract concept within the mind and yet be far removed from the 'reality of insight'. So there is the ظاھر the outward and superficial knowledge of a reality; and then there is the باطن which is the inner and esoteric knowledge of the same reality. Therefore he says, all knowledge contains a potential that calls out to be realized. But in order to realize that potential, one must penetrate the outer surface and dive deeper into concepts articulating the outward forms of that reality. That is how true knowledge of realities comes to life. 


So for example, we can have a superficial knowledge of a verse of Quran or a HADEES or a statement from نھج البلاغۃ. We can have a superficial understanding of why to pray and fast and how many prayers to do in a day, and how many  رکعات in each prayer etc. But then when you penetrate deep into the inner layers of these sayings of the Quran and HADEES. When you penetrate the exterior of صلواۃ to understand why 5 prayers? Why 17 رکعات in a day? Why one رکوع and two سجدۃ? What does the act of رکوع and سجدۃ truly represent? When you penetrate into these deeper esoteric layers of the باطن that's when a world of true knowledge emerges, that is very different than just a superficial knowledge of the same reality.


This is why the author says, Maulana Ali (AS) pointed to his chest when he said: هَاهُنَا لَعِلْماً جَمّاً . Here lies an ocean of knowledge. Because it is not the chest, but what lies hidden behind the chest, the heart, that contain knowledge? Chest is the outward form. It is the ظاھر, that contains and hides the heart within its باطن. But in order to reach the heart, one has to penetrate the chest. So what Maulana Ali (AS) points out here is that there is a difference between superficial knowledge and real knowledge. Superficial knowledge leads to factual information about something, whereas esoteric knowledge leads to a true recognition of reality and in turn leads to wisdom. But in order to do that, one must penetrate the surface of words, to dive deep into the various layers, to extract the treasures of its meanings. And the one who can help us do that is the true Imam of the time.

Assault

Now what's really interesting is the use of the word ھجم. It is generally translated as penetrate, but in reality ھجم is a term used in "war or battle" and it means 'to assault', 'to take by storm', 'to charge'. In those days when battle lines were formed, they would form a right wing, a left wing and a center called قلب الشکر - The heart of the army. And the goal of an assault would be to penetrate the left and right wings to reach the heart, where the king or the general of that army would stand. Once the king is captured, the battle is won. In fact this is how the chess board is arranged. So those who play chess understand these rules. 

So by using this word ھجم Maulana Ali (AS) is essentially pointing out to کمیل is that one has to conduct an assault on the exterior of the words, and penetrate them in order to reach the heart of its meaning that contains the true reality (حقیقۃ). There is a strive and an effort involved. It is not easy to get to that meaning. You see? What is that strive called? That strive to reach the true reality? It's called تحقیق - investigation. 


How does one do that? Through investigation (تحقیق). Investigation is the assault on words, leading to the depths of meaning, which is very different concept from a basic factual verification on the surface. Words can simply indicate or point to the true realities (the حقیقۃ) of religion, but not capture it. Just as the chest has to be literally opened up with a knife to reach the heart. 


Now what happens when one does penetrate the outer layers of words and reaches the true depths of reality? A shift in consciousness takes place. One begins seeing the same outer realities of this world differently from the rest of humanity who are only focused on the surface understanding of cause and effects. The deeper one dives, the more it shifts his consciousness and perceptions. This is why we see such a starch difference in the way some people view religion versus others; in the way some look at صلوۃ versus others. The some action is a burden on one, and a delight for the other. Why is that? It's the exact same action! It's because one is focused on the outward tiring form, whereas the other is focused on its true reality - its حقیقۃ. 


This is where you get the difference of نظر and بصر; the difference between sight and insight. Sight provides access to the outward superficial realities, whereas insight penetrates the surface plumbs out the deeper inner meanings to reveal its true reality. This is what Maulana Ali (AS) refers to as 'buried treasures': خزائن المدفونۃ. He says:

من فھم، علم گوہر العلم

The one who truly understood something, is the one who reached the depths of its knowledge. In another tradition he says:

بالتَّعَلُّم یَنَالَ العِلمْ

It is through learning and assimilation that knowledge is attained. In other words, learning the true inner realities of things require a tremendous effort, and only after it is assimilated, digested and rooted deeply within one's consciousness, one truly comes to know. That is when one moves from the abstract theories to the actual practices.


This concept is again effectively evoked by the use of the word ھجاما, which as I said, is related to battle. As I mentioned earlier, when an army breaks through the battle lines, and penetrates and breaks through the center hold to reach the heart قلب of that formation, they capture the king or the leader of that army. What happens then? The winner takes control over all the resources of the defeated army? They winner controls the kingdom, its resources, its treasury, its army, its countrymen. Right? Similarly when one knowledge penetrates the the surface of religion and takes control of the heart, a transformation begins to take place from deep within the soul. The heart, now illuminated with the true realities of religion,  takes control over the limbs, and actions; which are the resources of the soul. You see the analogy here? It's so incredible!  


Now what needs to be done once that knowledge of true reality is attained? It needs to be integrated within the soul. How? Through practice! In other words, this change in consciousness requires an effective integration of knowledge within the being itself. Outer knowledge needs to be penetrated to understand its true reality, and that awareness then needs to become a part of our consciousness in order to create spiritual transformation. It needs to be woven into the deepest texture and fabric of our existence. Therefore knowledge demands not just the attention of the mind but a complete orientation of the soul towards those realities. That is how spiritual transformation takes place.



We're studying the spiritual ethos of Maulana Ali (AS) to appreciate and learn from his remarkable perspective on religion. This is a continuation of his discourse to کمیل ابن زیاد on the virtues of knowledge and the people of knowledge. In the last session we looked at an analysis of the word ھجم used by Maulana Ali (AS) to describe an assault on words in order to penetrate and reach the depths of its meanings. We looked at the analogy of a battle, in which an army penetrates the enemy lines to capture the king, in order to take over his kingdom and his resources within his kingdom. This was compared to knowledge of realities penetrating and capturing the spiritual heart, which is considered the king of our human existence - because once the king (our heart) is overpowered, it becomes possible to take control of ones limbs and actions, that are the resources of its kingdom. We ended the previous conversation with the understanding that the end goal of this knowledge of realities, is to integrate it within the soul through practice. In other words, the exterior form of knowledge needs to be penetrated, deeply studied, they need to be taken to heart; in order to understand their true reality, thereby creating a shift in our consciousness; and once that shift takes place, to then integrate that awareness into our soul through practice. That is how spiritual transformation can be achieved.

{3:08} Requirements Tawheed

This is why the author states that knowledge demands not just the attention of the mind but a complete orientation of the soul. Because this goal of integrating knowledge within ones being cannot be achieved without the participation of the entire soul. And the spiritual term for this integration is توحید. The word واحد means the number one, and توحید therefore means "making one"; which in this context is the integration of knowledge with the soul. Making that knowledge of the true realities of Allah, one with the soul. That is the concept of توحید. 


Now how do we do that? What are the requirements of achieving this integration? Maulana Ali (AS) explains that it requires the elimination of all vices that flow from egocentricity. This idea of "Me, Myself & I". Because the نفس, the ego; is the root and the source of all vices.  Now one can argue, what is the problem with vices that do not harm anyone else? What if I have certain vices that only harm my own self? For example when I smoke, when I do drugs, when I drink alcohol, when I gamble - I'm not hurting anyone! So then what's wrong with that? 

Well first of all its a morality issue. Morality is defined as a person's standards of behavior or beliefs concerning what is acceptable; and what is not acceptable for them to do. Ethics on the other hand, is morality enforced by a society. Morality is enforced from inside by an individual; ethics is enforced from outside by a society. Morality can be subjective, because one can say, I don't find smoking or drinking immoral for myself. But ethics is objective, in that it dictates that you cannot infringe on the rights of others. So you can smoke and drink, but you cannot smoke in enclosed areas, and you cannot drive if you're drunk; because now you are infringing on the morality of others. That's ethics! 

Now let's understand the meaning of a vice. The dictionary simply defines a vice as an immoral or wicked behavior. But a more honest definition for a vice, is a wicked or immoral behavior that is targeted towards the pleasing of ones soul. Isn't it? That's why we engage in these vices, because it makes our soul happy; it makes "us" happy; it makes "Me, Myself & I" feel good. I smoke because it makes "me" feel good; I do drugs, because "I" like the high feeling. So vices are all about pleasing and satisfying the desires of  "Me, Myself & I". This is that 3rd group of people that Maulana Ali (AS) advised کمیل not to be from. This is the group he said was like بھیمۃ الانعام السامتۃ - dumb grazing cattle. Why? Because animals have no intellect. They live their entire life satisfying their urges and their instincts. They have no interest in recognizing their creator. They have no desire to recognize the purpose of their existence. So if that is the kind of life someone chooses for himself or herself, then this conversation does not apply to them in the first place; because you cannot reason with dumb cattle.

{8:46} Vices as obstacles

But if we are interested in living the life of an intellectual being; if we are interested in recognizing the true realities of our creator, then vices pose a serious threat to that goal. That is what the author is trying to explain here. He says that vices of the ego are a serious problem, because they are not just confined to the plane of morality; they encroach upon the domain of knowledge itself - because what is knowledge? Knowledge is defined as the clear and concrete vision of things. It is not simply a notion or conceptual understanding of something; it is actually a clear vision of a reality. Viewing things as they really are - that is knowledge! Now vices become a problem, because they act as these numerous clouds obscuring the vision proper to the 'reality of insight'. They act like thick layers of fog, diminishing visibility. Quran refers to these evil actions as 'rust' that covers up the heart:

Defrauding [83:14]
[ سورۃ ۸۳] المطففین
‏كَلَّا ۖ بَلْۜ رَانَ عَلَىٰ قُلُوبِهِم مَّا كَانُوا۟ يَكْسِبُونَ ‎۱۴
No! Rather, rust has covered their hearts of that which they were earning.

In other words, vices form layers of rust over the heart, making it impossible for one to achieve a true understanding of reality. This is why we find so many so-called intelligent and intellectual people, who find religion so difficult to deal with; and have numerous complains about religion. Why? These are highly intelligent people that can comprehend the intricacies of an atom, yet they cannot understand something as simple as the existence of God. It is because they are constantly engaged in fulfillment of the pleasures and delights of their egos. The desires of this world, their lust for دنیا forms layers of rust on their heart, eroding it and corrupting it, and thereby preventing them from understanding these spiritual realities - because the heart is the apparatus of insight.

This is why Maulana Ali (AS) describes the عاقل (a true intellectual) as the one who is constantly engaged in a 'struggle against his own soul'. He is the one who struggles against the lower inclinations of his soul; against his passionate whims, and the distracting caprices of his ego; because he understands that these vices will not only sully his soul, but they will literally blind his heart. In contrast, a virtuous person (متقی), opposes vice on the moral plane, simply because it is contrary to virtue. 

So now we find a distinction being made between the virtuous and the truly intelligent - between the متقی and the عاقل. The virtuous (متقی) opposes vices on the moral plane because he finds them contrary to virtue. But the عاقل not only opposes vices on the moral level, he transcends that plane of morality through this understanding, that these vices will prevent him from truly being able to witness the reality of Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala). You understand the difference? For the عاقل abstaining from vices is for a much greater reason than morality. For him, morality emerges as the fruits of his action from the seeds of spirituality. He sows the seeds of spirituality, and reaps the fruit of morality. You see? He strives for spirituality because morality is a natural outcome of that spirituality; whereas the متقی is merely focused on morality. So the rank of the true intellect (عاقل) is higher than someone who is simply virtuous.


But then he says something very interesting. He says vice is also opposed for another reason. A reason that is situated on a much higher level of spirituality - and this is a trait we find in the spiritual ethos of Maulana Ali (AS). The greatest and the ultimate reason to oppose vices, he says, is because they contradict the nature of the Real; Vices contradict the nature and the character of Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala) - because He created us in his image. The heart is the apparatus that enables us to experience his beatific vision, in his perfect form and glory. But when we rust our hearts with these vices, they obscures the reality of insight; and they corrupt the lens of our بصیرۃ. They blind the very 'eye' of our hearts, which alone is capable of witnessing and seeing Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala).


So now we have 3 levels at which vices and immoral acts are opposed. First is the level of the pious. They oppose vices because they are contrary to virtue. The second level is that of the intellectual. They oppose vices because of the understanding that vices will prevent him from truly being able to witness the reality of Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala). The third level is of the elite, who oppose vices because they find them contrary to the very nature of their creator.

{17:13} The Ultimate Struggle - Jehad Al-Akbar

Now what is the root cause of immorality? What is the essence of immorality? Maulana Ali (AS) states that immorality is rooted in ignorance. This is why, the uprooting of ignorance becomes the end goal of a true intellectual's existence; and the way he uproots this ignorance, is by dissolving the very source of his vices. What is the source of his vices? His ego! In other words, the intellectual focuses on keeping his ego under control which requires a tremendous effort. It requires a relentless battle within, which is, indeed the most formidable of all battles. This is why Rasul Allah(Salallahu alayhi wa aalihee wa sallam) defined this inner battle as جھاد الاکبر  the greater struggle. One day Rasul Allah(Salallahu alayhi wa aalihee wa sallam) was returning with his companions from a battle, and said 

رَجَعْنَا مِنَ الْجِھَادِ الْاَصْغَرِ اِلَی الْجِھَادِ الْاَکْبَرِ 

"We are returning from the lesser jihad (جھاد الاصغر) to the greater jihad (جھاد الاکبر)." When asked, "What is this greater jihad?  he replied:

ھُوَ جِھادٌ عَلی النَّفْس

"It is the struggle against oneself." So In other words, what he said is that the جھاد of sword is easier than the جھاد against the soul. Similarly, we find several statements by Maulana Ali (AS) about the importance of this inner struggle. He says:                                          

  • Struggle against the soul through knowledge - such is the mark of the intellect.
  • The strongest people are those who are strongest against their own souls.
  • Truly, the one who fights his own soul in obedience to Allah and does not sin against Him. He has the rank of the righteous martyr in the eyes of Allah.
  • The ultimate battle is that of a man against his own soul.
  • He who knows his soul fights it
  • No jihad is more excellent than the jihad of the soul.                                     

Now here is a perspective that I find really beautiful. Maulana Ali in clarifying what the Prophet meant by defining the greatest jihad as that of the soul; he says, it is not so much a question of fighting against the soul, but more of a battle for the soul itself. You see? The concept of جھاد الاکبر is not fighting against the soul, but fighting for the benefit of the soul. This emerges from a beautiful metaphor given by Maulana Ali (AS) to define this struggle: This is quite amazing! He says:

The intellectual العاقل is the leader of the forces of الرحمن. On the contrary الھوی  - caprice, whims and desires are commanded by the forces of الشیطان. So I want you to imagine these two opposing armies lined against each other. One one hand you have the armies of الرحمن and on the other you have the armies of الشیطان. OK?  Now what do armies need? They need a general! They need a leader, right? The intellect العقل is the leader, the commander in chief of the forces of الرحمن; while الھوی (caprice, whim and desire) is the leader of the forces of الشیطان. Our soul (our نفس) is that poor guy standing in between these two armies. Both generals armies are calling him, inviting him to join them. The عقل is inviting our soul to join the army of الرحمن; and الھوی is calling it to join the army of الشیطان. Our نفس is standing there vacillating  between both armies. It is susceptible to the attraction of both (مُتَجَاذِبَہ بینھما) and enters into 'the domain' of whichever of the two triumph. In other words, when confronted with vices, the intellect will battle desire, and whoever wins, will enlist the soul into his army. If the intellect wins, it will enlist the soul in the army of الرحمن , and if caprice wins, it will enlist the soul in the army of الشیطان. SubhanAllah! What a beautiful analogy to explain this concept. Now you can apply this to any situation in your life. It's time for صلوۃ but you're right in the middle of your job, or watching a movie, or whatever. The intellect will battle your desire in an attempt to recruit you in the army of الرحمن. Your desire to continue what you're doing will fight the intellect to enlist you in the army of الشیطان. You will end up in the army of whoever you listen to and allow victory. Sometimes just being conscious of this battle can help us make the right choices.

{25:47} Recruiting the soul

Then the author explains - that the purpose of these sayings is to highlight the fact, that for the true intellectual, the soul in its entirety has to be 'conquered' and enlisted in the army of الرحمن. So it is not a question of destroying the lower soul; but rather the idea of protecting the soul from the destructive influence of its caprice and its egoistic whims. Protecting it from the traps of subjectivism - this idea that knowledge is merely subjective and that there is no external or objective truth. Also protecting it from this notion of individualism - that we are independent and self-reliant beings and therefore don't need a god. All of these notions have been connoted by the term ھوی. Now term is used in the Quran, where it tells us that our Prophet did not speak from ھوی. 

Tha Star [53:3-4]
[ سورۃ ۵۳] النجم
‏وَمَا يَنطِقُ عَنِ ٱلْهَوَىٰٓ ‎۳‏إِنْ هُوَ إِلَّا وَحْىٌۭ يُوحَىٰ ‎۴
( 3 ) Nor does he speak of caprice. ( 4 ) It is only an Inspiration that he is inspired with

In other words, the divine objectivity of revelation, is being contrasted with the human subjectivity of passion and caprice, right? Revelation is objective whereas desires and caprice are subjective. The lesson in this verse is that the objectivity of the intellect must prevail over the subjectivity of ones caprice and egotistic desire. How does one do that? Through the intellect! It is the intellect, that brings to the forefront the divine power of الرحمن , and combats the forces of الشیطان, that are represented by الھوی. 


So why must one must battle with the ego. First because vice stands in opposition to virtue. This is the lowest level of morality. Then, because vices, cloud and obscure reality of Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala), making it impossible to view him as he truly is. But then the greatest reason of all, is that vices contradict the nature of the Real (الحق). Allah created us in his image, and the greatest injustice we can do, is tarnish the true beauty of that image through the stains of our vices. Because once that happens, not only are we unable to see God, our perceptions get distorted to the point that we begin seeing ourselves as god. This is why the Quran states that the desires stemming from ego, turns it into a kind of false god:

The Criterion [25:43]
[ سورۃ ۲۵] الفرقان
‏أَرَءَيْتَ مَنِ ٱتَّخَذَ إِلَهَهُۥ هَوَىٰهُ ‎۴۳
Have you seen the one who takes as his god his own desire?

As I said earlier, vices are mechanisms for the ego to feel good. You take out one "o" from good, what does it become? It becomes god! This is how we constantly create gods, in the name of feeling "good". This is the subtle or hidden idolatry (الشرک الخفی) that Rasul Allah(Salallahu alayhi wa aalihee wa sallam) referred to as being more hidden than a black ant crawling on a black stone in a dark moonless night. In order to eliminate this idolatry of the ego, a force higher and stronger than that of the ego has to be engaged. Right? Because it is impossible for the ego to be controlled or dominated by something pertaining to the ego itself. So then what is this higher force? It is the divine Reality, who is made accessible through the power of the intellect and the graces of faith. In other words, they both complement each other. The more we struggle to overcome our vices, our desires, our ھوی that stem from our ego, the more we will feel the influx of Allah's divine grace within our soul. This is again expressed by the Quran as:

Those Who Drag Forth [79:40-41]
[ سورۃ ۷۹] النازیات
‏وَأَمَّا مَنْ خَافَ مَقَامَ رَبِّهِۦ وَنَهَى ٱلنَّفْسَ عَنِ ٱلْهَوَىٰ ‎۴۰‏فَإِنَّ ٱلْجَنَّةَ هِىَ ٱلْمَأْوَىٰ ‎۴۱
( 40 ) But as for him who feared standing before his Lord, and restrained himself from impure evil desires, and lusts. ( 41 ) Verily, Paradise will be his abode. 

So what we learn from Maulana Ali (AS) is that soul's fundamental energy is not to be destroyed, but rather converted and redirected away from the transient objects of personal desire. The goal is to move away from the impulses of شیطان  towards the one true being that is expressed by الرحمن. This 'conquest' of the soul involves a lot more than subscribing to some mental concept of God. If one desires to be able to perceive that truth in all its dimensions and all its ramifications on all the different planes of existence, then it is not just the mind; it is not just the will; it is not just the character, but the fundamental disposition of the soul that needs to be integrated into the truth- the truth which can only be perceived by the intellect.

                   

Now what's important to understand is that Maulana Ali's need to win this soul over in its totality, comes from his understanding of توحید, which is the most fundamental principle of Islam. The word توحید is a noun as well as a verb. It is an entity all by itself, but then it is also an action. Let's first understand توحید in light of both these grammatical forms. 


As a noun, the word توحید  is simply translated as the oneness or the unity of Allah. But literally, it means 'affirming the one'. It is the affirmation of and belief, in the oneness of Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala). That he is alone, he is unique and has no partners. This is the outward, theological meaning of the word. Another way of looking at توحید  is this idea of 'realizing the one' - the realization that there is but one reality. In other words, Allah is the only reality that exists. Everything else in comparison to the reality of Allah is merely an illusion. Because it is the nature of reality to persist, whereas it is the nature of illusion to fade away. Everything and everyone in this universe, including the universe itself will fade away into non-existence, but the essence of Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala) will persist and remain forever.

The Beneficent [55:26-27]
[ سورۃ ۵۵] الرحمن
‏كُلُّ مَنْ عَلَيْهَا فَانٍۢ ‎۲۶‏وَيَبْقَىٰ وَجْهُ رَبِّكَ ذُو ٱلْجَلَلِ وَٱلْإِكْرَامِ ‎۲۷
( 26 ) Whatsoever is on it will perish. ( 27 ) And the Face of your Lord full of Majesty and Honour will abide forever. 

This is the inner spiritual meaning of the word توحید. Now, in Maulana Ali's perspective, the absolutely Real (الحق) is perceived not only as being one (as a noun), but also as making one, or integrating  (as a verb). In other words the Real is not only unique, he is also unifying. With this understanding توحید becomes not just affirming the notion of the oneness of Allah but also "being" one with him, in order to reflect his perfection through practice. In other words, to truly know Allah, is to be 'integrated' with his reality. To truly know Allah is to behave and act like Allah. This is why there is a حدیث القدسی in which Allah says:

أتخذو من أخلاق اللہ

Adopt the traits and characteristics of Allah. Become one with the character of Allah. Be like the one whose image you have been created in. This is why when we study the سیرۃ of Rasul Allah(Salallahu alayhi wa aalihee wa sallam) and Maulana Ali (AS), we truly come to know of Allah, because they both beautifully reflected and demonstrated the characteristics of Allah in a practical form. By their character, their demeanor, their love, their justice, their compassion, their mercy, their generosity, their forgiveness, their courage, people accepted and submitted to their creator and master. Because if the creation is so incredibly amazing, then how incredible, and how amazing must their creator be - SubhanAllah! 



We're studying the spiritual ethos of Maulana Ali (AS). This is a continuation of his discourse to کمیل ابن زیاد on the virtues of knowledge and the people of knowledge. So far we've looked at the methodology of acquiring knowledge described by the word ھجم used by Maulana Ali (AS) to describe an assault on words in order to penetrate the depths of its meanings. We learnt that the purpose of knowledge is to integrate it within the soul through practice in order to create a shift in our consciousness so that spiritual transformation can be achieved. We looked at the difference between morality and ethics, and how vices pose as obstacles in the path of our spiritual transformation. We discussed the ultimate struggle of جھاد الاکبر  which is for the intellect to win over caprice and enlist the soul in the army of الرحمن. We ended the conversation on the concept of توحید being a noun as well as a verb, which is the idea of integrating with Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala) in understanding as well as in action, exemplified for us in the lives of our prophet and Maulana Ali (AS).

{2:01} Spiritual Heart & Intellect

They both are like the spiritual heart and the intellect for the soul of humanity. In other words, if we look at all of humanity as one single entity, as one single soul, then Muhammad and Ali are its heart and intellect, created for its salvation. You see? The spiritual heart is what perceives and experiences the reality of Allah, whereas the intellect recognizes that reality as the truth through logical reasoning. The word عقل as we know, means to bind or restrain. Its job is to define and enclose meaning, On the other hand, the heart works based on intuition, inspiration; and its job is to discloses spiritual truth. In other words, all the realities that the heart witnesses and experiences, the intellects 'binds them together', into the organic unity of توحید through reason, which is an exercise in logic - negation and affirmation; constriction and expansion.


This principle is expressed in the following statements of Maulana Ali, each of which requires deep reflection. He says:

مَنْ عَرَفَ نَفسُہُ تَجَرَّدُ
مَنْ عَرَفَ النَّاسَ تَفَرَّدُ
مَنْ عَرَفَ الدُّنیاَ تَزَہَّدُ
مَنْ عَرَفَ اللہ تَوَحَّدُ

Before we look at these statements, we must first understand the meaning of the word عرف. There are two words َعَلِم and َعَرَف. The word Maulana Ali (AS) used here is not عَلِم but عَرَف. So let's first understand the difference between the two words. The word عَلِم means to gain knowledge or to understand the factual reality of something. The word عَرَف is a higher form of understanding which has to do with recognizing something's essence; or recognizing the reality of something in its entirety without any obscurity. To have a perfectly clear vision of something. علم can be partial and incomplete, but عرف is perfect and complete. The word اعراف means heights, or "high ground", and it comes from the same root word. What happens when you reach a higher ground? You gain a clear view of the sky as well as land. You gain a perfect view of the terrain. So عَرَف means knowledge with complete and perfect clarity.  Now with this understanding let's look at these sayings of Maulana Ali (AS). 

مَنْ عَرَفَ نَفسُہُ تَجَرَّدُ

He who truly recognizes the essence of his soul disengages himself. The one who witnesses the ugliness, the triviality, and the flaws of ones desires, caprices and whims - runs away from it. Because it is within human nature to repel the ugly and the fowl. Then he says:

مَنْ عَرَفَ النَّاسَ تَفَرَّدُ

He who truly recognizes the essence of people, isolates himself. Because as Saint Augustine said: "Hell is other people". They are a never ending ocean of demands and expectation that can never be fulfilled. People are disappointments waiting to happen. We're talking about people who are a slave of their own whims and caprice. This is why the word ناس is used - common folk. Then he says:

مَنْ عَرَفَ الدُّنیاَ تَزَہَّدُ

He who truly knows the essence of this world withholds himself from it. He comes to recognize what a dangerous place this world is with its temptations and distractions. Why? Because it distracts us and takes us away from our creator. So the one recognizes that reality withholds himself from it. Then he says:

مَنْ عَرَفَ اللہ تَوَحَّدُ

He who truly recognizes Allah integrates himself with his creator.


If we contemplate on these statements, we see that Maulana Ali (AS) mentions three entities of disengagement or disassociation. The self, people, and دنیا . Then he mentions one entity of integration which is Allah - مَنْ عَرَفَ اللہ تَوَحَّدُ - He who truly recognizes Allah integrates himself with him; he becomes like him. The key point to understand here, is that one cannot reach the stage of integration with Allah, without first disengaging from the self; from people and from this ephemeral world. And one cannot disengage from these 3 traps without realizing their true essence.

This does not mean becoming a hermit. He's talking about a spiritual disassociation. The goal is to live in this world among everyone, but to make Allah our ultimate desire. This is function of the intellect. So you can see how the spiritual heart recognizes the reality of Allah(Subhanahu wa Ta'ala), but the intellect then binds and defines it all together through the process of disassociation and association.


Now what's really interesting, is that when a person integrates himself with Allah, that very shift in his consciousness and character elevates his self, and makes him beloved to people and to this world. How hard do we all try to be loved by people and fit into this world? In society? In school? At work? The more we try, the more we are rejected by both. But according to Maulana Ali (AS), once we disengage from them, and integrate with Allah; once we become like Allah, the natural outcome of that shift in consciousness and character, is that we will be loved by both. Rasul Allah(Salallahu alayhi wa aalihee wa sallam) and Maulana Ali (AS) are the two perfect examples of this principle in action, who were loved by not just friends but also by their enemies; not just 1400 years ago, but throughout time to this very day! SubhanAllah! 

{12:08} Sincerity

Maulana Ali (AS) refers to this total adherence to the Real as 'sincerity', صدق or أخلاص. What does اخلاص mean? The term اخلاص generally means truthfulness or sincerity. But the root word خَلُصَ means to be utterly pure. It means to be true to one's essence; to be unadulterated by any extraneous substance. Maulana Ali says:

ُأَخْلَصُ للہِ العِلم وَ العَمَل وَالحُبْ وَ العَدٰوَۃ، وَالقَبْضُ و المَغَادِرَۃ، وَالنُّطْقُ وَ الصُّمْت

Make purely for Allah your action and your knowledge, your love and your hatred, your taking and your leaving, and your speech and your silence


To be totally sincere, means to make oneself, and ones desires 'purely' for Allah, without any stain of impurity. What is impurity in this context? A desire for anything that is other than Allah present in one's devotion; in ones action; in ones intention and every other sentiment. One must love and hate only in function of one's relationship with Allah. Of all the tendencies, egotism is the most harmful sentiment that insinuate itself within one's motivations and intentions.
